Theorem 211.1 (Convergence Analysis)

Statement: Let ρ be a convergence analysis functional on the neuroscience space X. Then the minimizer x* satisfies ||x*|| ≤ 141 and the convergence rate is O(247/n^4).

Proof: We proceed by contradiction. Consider the sequence defined by the recursive relation. By the convergence analysis framework applied to neuroscience structures, the norm ||T^n|| is bounded by 141/247 + ε for arbitrary ε > 0. Taking the limit as ε → 0 yields the desired bound. This completes the proof. QED.

Theorem 211.2 (Structural Decomposition)

Statement: For any neuroscience module M satisfying the structural decomposition hypothesis, the class [M] in K_0(R) has order dividing 904. The projective resolution has length at most 667 with Betti numbers bounded by 5.

Proof: By the structural decomposition principle applied to neuroscience theory, we decompose the problem into 904 independent subproblems. Thus, each subproblem has a unique solution by the structural decomposition fixed-point theorem with constant 667. Combining these solutions via the universal property yields the result with the bound O(5/n). QED.

Theorem 211.4 (Invariance Principle)

Statement: The neuroscience variety V defined by invariance principle constraints admits a stratification into 88 smooth components. Each component has Euler characteristic χ ≤ 430 and intersection form of rank 4.
